京元電子 (2449 TT) 
狼來了，狼還沒來

永豐觀點 
下修 4Q26 營運展望，以反映持續的來料不順。 
 投資評價與建議 
目標價維持 320 元：京元電短期逆風未平，然 2027 年將迎來強勁成長，係考量
(1) 年底為 CPU、網通客戶等增加的產能，可自 1Q27 起完整貢獻營收，(2) 京元
電有機會增加在主要客戶 TPU 的市占率，目前已在和客戶討論下世代晶片搭配的
預燒爐，(3) HPC 新品放量，採用液冷預燒爐帶動測試單價提升。惟須留意產能快
速擴張下的成本增加。評價面，以2027 年修正後 EPS 推算目前 PER 約 23.3x，考
量 AI/HPC 相關需求未見軟弱，建議拉回再行佈局，目標價 320 元(25.2x 2027 
EPS(F))。

ESG 評析 
京元電企業永續評鑑整體為 B+等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面向
平均得分分別為 B+等與 B+等。 
SinoPac+ ESG 評鑑系統評等

資料來源：SinoPac+ ESG 評等 (依循 2023 SinoPac+企業永續評鑑方法學) 
註 1：資誠永續發展服務股份有限公司僅於ＥＳＧ評鑑系統方法學建置過程中，就評分指標提供
專業意見，對於評分結果及評估報告內容之完整性及真實性，不負擔保責任，亦不對閱讀或使用
本評估報告之第三方負任何責任。 
註 2：永豐投顧已獲 SASB 授權，於研究中使用 SASB 重大性地圖之一般議題分類及揭露主題。

京元電為國內 IC 測試大廠：成立於 1987 年，主要提供前段晶圓測試(CP)及後段
IC 成品測試(FT)、預燒等服務，近年透過自製測試設備增加取得 AI 客戶信任，同
時降低封裝業務來改善公司平均獲利能力。 2Q26 營收產品組合為晶圓測試
29.6%、產品測試 61%、產品預燒 7.7%、封裝 1.3%、其他 0.4%。應用別營收比
重為消費性電子 30.3%、通訊 17.5%、數據處理與儲存 36.3%、汽車電子 13.0%、
工業 2.5%、其他 0.4%。目前主要生產據點位於桃園楊梅、苗栗竹南、苗栗頭份、
苗栗銅鑼廠區，並正在新加坡、美國等地進行佈局。主要客戶包含全球主要半導體
公司，如聯發科、韋爾半導體、AMD(Xilinx)、意法半導體等，主要競爭對手為日
月光、矽格等。 
美國廠放量或在 2028 年後 ：研究部預期台積電美國先進封裝廠區將於 2H28-2029
年進入量產，京元電尚未公布美國廠的具體時程與選址，但我們認為 AeternZ 廠
址存在落腳德州的可能性，理由有三：(1) 下游組裝廠已在當地佈局，(2) 亞利桑
那州須與晶圓廠爭搶技術人力，(3) 京元電位處後段測試，靠近終端組裝較符合供
應鏈配置。另外，本次公告的客戶預付款，我們認為宣示意義大於實質挹注，係因
封測廠資本支出以設備為大宗 ， 而該客戶的測試設備多已採託管(consignment)模
式，京元電本身需投入的設備資金不高，預付款對降低資本支出壓力的效果有限。
此外 ，京元電赴美設廠短期內本就以服務少數關鍵客戶為主 ， 此公告屬預期內的進
展，我們不建議過度解讀。 
來 料 不 如 預 期 ， 下 修 短 期 展 望 ：研 究 部 下修 京 元 電4Q26 營收至 131 億
(+8.4%QoQ，+32.0%YoY， 原估143 億)， 主要考量(1) 上游來料不順 ， 造成AI/HPC
相關產品放量幅度不如預期，而對應的 CPU 出貨則穩定墊高，(2) 客戶下修非旗
艦機款封測需求。獲利部分同樣因營收放大不如預期、新產能開出，抵銷掉少了夏
季電費的正向影響，預估毛利率 39.2%，稅後淨利 29.8 億(+14.9%QoQ，
+32.8%YoY)，EPS 2.32 元。 
2027 年展新機：目前我們並未見到 AI/HPC 終端需求轉弱的跡象，因此京元電業
績拉升僅是時間問題，預期 2027 年受惠(1) 年底為 CPU、網通等客戶增加的產
能，可自 1Q27 起完整貢獻營收，(2) 京元電有機會增加在主要客戶 TPU 的市站
率，目前已在和客戶討論下世代晶片搭配的預燒爐，(3) HPC 新品放量，採用液冷
預燒爐帶動測試單價提升。從供給端來看，楊梅廠、頭份一廠、頭份二廠的無塵室
在 1H27 陸續開出後，我們認為2027 年京元電的成長將不再受到產能限制，而是
看客戶的拉貨節奏。 
財務預估：研究部預估京元電 2026 年營收 466 億(+33.5%YoY)，毛利率 39.1%，
稅後淨利 103.5 億(-6.0%YoY，年減主因 2025 年公司處分蘇州廠房)，EPS 8.07
元(原估 8.81 元)。2027 年營收 682 億(+46.3%YoY)，毛利率 41.1%，稅後淨利
163.2 億(+57.6%YoY)，EPS 12.72 元(原估 13.93 元)。

Greater China Technology Hardware  | Asia Pacific
Higher OE Estimates, Upgrade
Largan and Genius
Following our semi team's increase in optical engine (OE)
estimates, we raise our fiber array unit (FAU) estimates
accordingly and thus upgrade Largan to OW and Genius to EW.
Morgan Stanley Asia Limited+
Andy Meng, CFA
Equity Analyst
Andy.Meng@morganstanley.com                                               +852 2239-7689
Betty Chen
Research Associate

Betty.H.Chen@morganstanley.com                                               +852 2239-7213
Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
What’s Changed
Genius Electronic Optical Co.
Ltd. (3406.TW) From To
Rating Underweight Equal-weight
Price Target NT$910.00 NT$1,070.00
Largan Precision (3008.TW) From To
Rating Equal-weight Overweight
Price Target NT$7,100.00 NT$9,040.00
Raising FAU estimates after upstream semi team's OE estimates changes: Our OE

shipment forecasts increase by 128%/52% to 17.73mn/73.87mn in 2027/28 (see full
report). As OE and FAU are 1:1, we raise our FAU volume forecasts accordingly, while
key other assumptions are unchanged, which resulted in an FAU TAM increase  from
US$778mn to US$1.773bn in 2027e and from US$4.86bn to US$7.387bn in 2028e.
This significant FAU TAM increase leads to the changes below.
We now value Largan at NT$9,040/share, or 36% upside potential: Our analysis

suggests that Largan's share price already embeds >30% global market share in FAU
before formal production starts in 2H27 or even 2028. But our revised assumptions
suggest further upside if Largan successfully ramps up production and gains
meaningful market share. To reflect 3Q earnings miss, we trim our intrinsic value
estimates from NT$3,000 to NT$2,800 per share for the company's traditional
business, while our TAM analysis derives a value of NT$6,240/share  for CPO/FAU

new business, bringing the price target to NT$9,040. Considering the stronger
growth potential in 2028, we upgrade the stock to OW from EW.
We now value Genius at NT$1,070/share, upgrade to EW: Similarly, we believe
Genius's share price is also affected by hopes for FAU components.  Using a similar
approach, we conclude that the aggregate value is NT$1,070/share (NT$600 for
existing business and NT$470/share for the FAU component business). We therefore

see a more balanced risk/reward and hence move to EW.
Key risks to our call: While upstream semi capacity and yield might have already
improved, we believe the yield of FAU coupling/matching will be a key overhang.
Any lower-than-expected yield could result in  shipment misses for FAU and OE. If
the yield is not ideal, key customers are less likely to place large orders despite
upstream capacity being ready. On the upside, earlier-than-expected mass

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
We trim Largan's traditional business value to NT$2,800/share but raise FAU
business to NT$6,240/share, from which we derive a new PT of NT$9,040.
We value Genius's traditional business at NT$600/share and FAU business at NT
$470/share, from which we derive a new PT of NT$1,070.
We are more positive on FAU's TAM but, we think FAU's coupling/matching could

be an overhang to our higher OE/FAU shipment estimates in 2027-28.
October 12, 2026 09:00 PM GMT

Idea

Executive Summary
Higher optical engine shipment assumptions as yields
improve
Our semi team has lifted their optical engine shipment assumptions following their latest
supply-chain checks. While PIC production remains a bottleneck, improving SoIC yields
and downstream assembly yields should support a faster OE ramp. They now expect OE
production to reach 2mn/59mn/148mn units in 2026/27/28, with actual OE shipments
reaching 0.39mn/17.73mn/73.87mn units, respectively. In particular, they assume the SoIC

yield improves from 50% in 2026 to 95% in 2027 onwards, while downstream assembly
yield rises from 20% in 2026 to 30%/50% in 2027/28. We believe this provides greater
confidence in the CPO volume ramp, although downstream assembly and testing remain
key gating factors for how quickly OE shipments can scale.

Largan: Larger CPO/FAU upside; upgrade to OW
Based on the semi team's assumption revision, we lift our CPO optical engine volume
assumptions for 2027-28e while keeping other assumptions unchanged:
1. We forecast CPO optical engine volume to increase from 0.39mn in 2026 to
17.73mn in 2027, with a further surge to 73.87mn in 2028. Consequently, FAU
(Fiber Array Units – vital optical interconnect components that align multiple

optical fibers with silicon chips, making them critical for Co-Packaged Optics)
volumes for CPO also grow from 0.39mn to 73.87mn in the same period;
2. We assume FAU ASP of US$100 in 2026-28 (average market level);
3. We forecast Largan's FAU market share to increase from 0% in 2026 to 5% in
2027 and 40% in 2028.
4. Assuming net margins of 10% in 2027 and 30% in 2028, we forecast FAU earnings
of US$9mn in 2027 and US$882mn in 2028.

M
Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 3
If we value the FAU business at 35x 2028e P/E (industry peers are at 40-50x P/E, but we
use a more conservative multiple to reflect the first year of scalable production), this
implies an incremental market cap of US$30.862bn. Discounting this back to 2026 at a
10% rate, we derive a per share positive impact of NT$6,240.

3Q26 results miss; revising down traditional business estimates. Largan reported 3Q26

revenue of NT$15.65bn, down 11% YoY, with gross margin declining to 42.2% and net
income of NT$5.18bn, down 27% YoY. The weaker-than-expected gross margin, mainly due
to lower new-product yields and higher structural green-power costs, resulted in a
meaningful earnings miss. Together with softer smartphone order momentum, we lower
our 2026-28 revenue estimates by 5-6% and gross margin assumptions to
45.8%/44.5%/47.5%, resulting in 12%/16%/9% downward revisions to our 2026-28

earnings estimates, respectively. For more details, please refer to Earnings Estimate
Revision                  section.
We remain positive on Largan's CPO/FAU opportunity given the company's strong track
record in high-precision manufacturing relative to most Taiwan peers. Following our semi
team's higher optical engine shipment assumptions, we see a meaningfully larger FAU
addressable market in 2027-28. More recently, Largan indicated that its first FA project

has effectively passed customer verification and that it is expanding from FA into FAU,
including coupling and potential FAU-to-carrier assembly, with dedicated capacity
expected to support mass production from around mid-2027/2H27. While the current
share price already reflects some CPO/FAU expectations, our revised assumptions suggest
further upside if Largan successfully ramps production and gains meaningful market share.
We set our price target at NT$9,040, comprising NT$2,800 from the traditional business

and NT$6,240 from the CPO/FAU business.
Genius: Higher CPO/FAU optionality; upgrade to EW

### Page 4

M
Idea
4
Similar to Largan, following semi-team's higher assumptions, we also lift our CPO optical
engine volume assumptions for 2027-2028e while keeping other assumptions unchanged.
1. We apply the same FAU volume and ASP assumptions as we do for Largan.
2. We expect Genius to be involved in FAU components, rather than overall
solutions; therefore, we assume components TAM to be 15% of the FAU TAM.
3. We forecast Genius's market share to increase from 0% in 2026 to 5% in 2027 and

30% in 2028.
4. Assuming net margins of 10% in 2027 and 25% in 2028, we forecast FAU
component earnings of US$1mn in 2027 and US$81mn in 2028.
If we value the FAU component business at 25x 2028e P/E (lower multiple than Largan
because Genius has smaller scale), it could introduce incremental market cap of US
$2,004mn. Discounting it back to 2026 at a 10% discounted rate, we derive a per-share
positive impact of NT$470.

Similar to Largan, our residual income model derives an intrinsic value of NT$600/share
for the traditional business, while the TAM analysis suggests CPO/FAU-related
opportunities could be valued at NT$470/share. Adding these together, we raise our price
target materially to NT$1,070.
Given Largan's traditional business has faced margin headwinds in 3Q26 due to the
application of green power, it's reasonable to expect that Genius could also face similar

customer requirements on green power. However, as Genius has more production capacity
in mainland China, we believe it could face less significant cost pressure. We therefore
maintain our estimates unchanged at this stage and will monitor margin development in
the upcoming quarterly results.

Earnings Estimate Revision
Largan reported 3Q26 revenue of NT$15.65bn (-11% YoY) and net income of NT$5.18bn (-
27% YoY), with gross margin declining to 42.2%. Following weaker-than-expected 3Q26
results, softer smartphone order momentum and a lower gross margin outlook due to
new-product yield pressure and higher green-power costs, we lower our revenue and GPM
estimates for 2026-28, resulting in 9-16% downward earnings revisions over the period.

Valuation and Price Target Revisions
Largan: For the traditional business, we lower our earnings and medium-term growth rate;
as a result, our intrinsic value in our base case scenario is adjusted down to NT$2,800
from NT$3,000. We use RIM valuation methodology for Greater China Hardware Tech
companies in our coverage. We apply an unchanged CoE of 8.5%, a medium-term growth
rate of 8% (from 7% previously on higher 2026e estimates), and a long-term growth rate

of 3% (unchanged). However, we believe the upward revision to optical engine shipment
assumptions materially increases the addressable market and earnings potential for
Largan's FAU business. Therefore, we add the base case per share positive value from FAU
based on the analysis in the previous section. The new SOTP valuation derives a new price
target at NT$9,040 (from NT$7,100). We also raise our bull case and bear case values to
NT$11,426 (from NT$11,142.80)  and NT$4,620 (from NT$3,649.20).

Genius: For the traditional business, we make no earnings and intrinsic value revisions; as
a result, our intrinsic value under our base case scenario is unchanged at NT$600. We use
RIM valuation methodology for Greater China Hardware Tech companies in our coverage.
We apply a CoE of 8.5%, a medium-term growth rate of 4%, and a long-term growth rate
of 3%. However, we believe the upward revision to optical engine shipment assumptions
increases the potential value of Genius's FAU opportunity, although execution visibility

remains lower than for more established suppliers. Therefore, we add the base case per
share positive value from FAU based on the analysis in the previous section. The new
SOTP valuation derives a new price target at NT$1,070 (from NT$910). We also raise our
bull case and bear case values to NT$1,834 (from NT$1,705.20) and NT$658 (from NT
$592.40).

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

嘉澤：全球前三大 CPU Socket 供應商，伺服器已成營收主軸
全球前三大中央處理器插座CPU socket供應商。依Bishop統計在電腦暨週邊連
接器廠商中全球排名第四，不分行業別的全球連接器排名全球排名第十八。
主要客戶為AMD與Intel。產品涵蓋 CPU Socket、記憶體插槽、高速連接器及
線材，並延伸至 SOCAMM、液冷快接頭與 NPO Socket。
本研究預估，嘉澤伺服器營收由 2026 年221.14 億元增至 2027 年 328.67 億元，
年增 48.6%。成長來自板量回升、CPU新平台提高 Socket 與高速連接器價值，
以及 AI 新產品放量；PC 相關營收占比則由 2025 年 36.60%，降至 2027 年
16.5%，營運重心持續轉向伺服器。

CPU socket是甚麼
CPU 插槽的設計目的是將 CPU 處理器牢固地固定在主機板上，並在設備移動時
有助於防止潛在的損壞。嘉澤主要客戶為AMD與Intel。
資料來源 :premioinc.com
PGA (插針網格陣列)BGA (球柵陣列)LGA (平面網格陣列)項目
針腳在CPU上，插入主
機板的孔位中。
CPU 底部是微小錫球，直
接高溫焊接在主機板上。
CPU 底部是平貼金屬觸點，
針腳做在主機板插槽上。外觀結構
可以。直接拉起拉桿即
可拔插更換。
不行。屬於永久焊接，需

卸。
可以。可自由解鎖扣具、更
換或升級 CPU。更換/升級
1. 針腳在 CPU 上，輕
微彎曲時通常還能手動
扳正修復。
1. 體積最薄、最省空間。
適合行動裝置。
1.風險轉移：安裝失誤時損
壞的是主機板，CPU 價值
較高(倍數差距)

的機率幾乎是零。
2. 傳輸路徑最短，高頻與

散熱表現優異。
2.高密度高上限：相同面積
內可容納更多觸點，能支援
更多通道與更高供電，技術
天花板最高。
效能上限受限：針腳物
理體積大，無法做到高
密度排列，高頻與多通
道（如 DDR5）訊號完
整性不如 LGA。

永久焊接，壞任何一個晶
片通常需要更換整張主機
板。
主機板針腳極度脆弱：主機
板插槽針腳如毛髮般細小，
清理或安裝時極易壓歪，且
幾乎無法手動修復。

代理 AI 讓 CPU 工作量增加：從回覆問題，走向執行任務
原先：用戶提出問題；應用程式向模型發送提示；模型生成答案；應用程式將答
案傳回給用戶。主節點CPU負責調度、I/O和系統管理，而GPU則負責繁重的運
算任務。
現在：代理AI不再只是回答一個問題，而是將目標分解成多個步驟，決定下一步
該做什麼，調用多個模型，查詢資料庫，連接API，運行企業應用程序，檢查權
限，檢索內存，驗證輸出，然後再次循環執行整個過程。
CPU負責：編排：管理分解複雜任務的引擎。代理執行和工具呼叫：觸發 API 和
傳統企業軟體。策略與安全：對每個自主操作進行實際檢查。
隨 AI 從模型推論延伸至 Agent 執行，CPU 的角色擴及任務協調、工具操作及資
料處理。AMD 5 月官方文章已描述 CPU：GPU 由過去約 1：4–8，朝 1：1 移
動；Intel Q2 法說進一步表示，目前以顆數計已接近均衡。兩家原廠的觀察，支
持 CPU 在 AI 基礎建設中重要性提高

資料來源 :AMD官網(智能體人工智慧改變了CPU/GPU的平衡)
5

AI 基礎設施正在從「以 GPU 為中心」的架構，走向「GPU 與 CPU 分工協
作」的分散式架構。未來不是只增加 GPU，而是新增獨立的 Agentic CPU
compute layer
New Agentic CPU Racks：新增的 Agent 執行層負責 Agent Control
Plane 與 Tool Execution管理 agent 的任務拆解、工作流調度、狀態管理與
多 agent 協作執行 API 呼叫、SQL 查詢、文件檢索、資料處理、程式執行與
外部工具調用處理權限控管、稽核紀錄、 安全檢查、重試與人工審批核心功
能是把 LLM 的「決策」轉換成企業系統中的「實際行動」

Meta Muse：個人代理產品化，使 CPU 執行環境的需求更具體
Muse 的投資意義在於：大型消費平台開始提供可持續執行任務的個人代理，使
CPU 沙盒需求從技術架構走向實際服務。
與AMD的關係Meta已部署數百萬顆AMD EPYC，並是Venice、Verano 的領先
客戶。因此，Meta 的運算擴張是 AMD CPU 需求的重要觀察對象。
持續在線的虛擬機（Persistent VMs）： Meta Muse 的底層採用了 Muse
Secure VM 架構。這些 VM 即使在使用者沒有主動點開 App 時，也在後台持續
運作，消耗大量的作業系統配置、記憶體管理與安全驗證資源—這些全部屬於通
用運算，只有 CPU 能做。因此當億萬名用戶的 AI 代理同時在線，CPU 的短缺
會遠比 GPU 來得更強烈。Muse 這樣的個人代理規模不斷擴大，對 CPU 的需求
勢必會增長，預計 CPU 與 GPU 的比例將從 1:1往4:1以上走 (intel電話會議)

資料來源 :trendforce, meta
對運算需求的意義Muse 是甚麼
大型平台投入代理服務產品
化
2026 年 9 月 8 日推出個人
AI 代理
執行環境需要 CPU、記憶體
及系統資源

工具操作與程式執行增加具備瀏覽器，可跨應用處理工作

Meta muse帶動AI agent趨勢，使CPU類股上漲
 Meta Muse 在 9 月 8 日剛上線時，市場仍在觀望它與傳統的問答機器人（如 ChatGPT）
有何不同。直到 9 月 18 日（上線第 10 天），第三方研調機構 Sensor Tower 釋出數據，
顯示 Muse 已經連續數天登頂美國 App Store 免費榜冠軍，13 天內累計下載突破 250 萬
次。這時市場才驚覺，這是一個「現象級爆款」。
 Muse 可以實際幫用戶串接 Gmail、自動比價，甚至與 Shopify 的 Shop Pay 整合代客結
帳。當週一（9 月 21 日）開盤，市場資金首先推升 Meta 股價大漲 11%。CPU類股全面
大漲10%，嘉澤隔日也開盤即漲停，顯示市場有定位上的連動。(如下圖)
 截至9/30，目前該應用僅在美國與加拿大上線2 2天突破了 500 萬次下載。相比之下，
ChatGPT 達到 500 萬次下載花了 56 天，Grok 花了 103 天，Claude 則耗時 492 天。市

場發現 AI 正從「回答問題」走向「替人類實際操作」的實際應用，引發了 AI Agent的邁
向普及的轉折點。
資料來源 :google財經、yahoo股市

CPU 需求同時受惠於通用伺服器與 AI 代理
代理 AI 提高 CPU 需求，但嘉澤面對的市場不只 AI 機櫃；通用伺服器仍
是重要出貨基礎。
DIGITIMES觀察，Agentic AI促使伺服器架構提高CPU與GPU配置比例，

開發NPO socket，為未來潛在商機
資料來源 :moneyDJ、利成光電科技有限公司
圖片來源：OIF 的《Co-Packaging Framework》第 10 頁、圖 2(d) 21
NPO的核心定義是：將光引擎從傳統的前面板可插拔位置，移動到更靠近交
換晶片（ASIC） 的地方，但光引擎和ASIC仍然是兩個獨立的封裝體。它們通
常被並排安裝在同一塊高性能基板（如矽中介層或有機基板）上
NPO Socket已進入客戶最後認證階段，且達可量產狀態，量產目標落在2027
年第3季，目前未納入估計，等待未來公司給更多指引。
AI資料中心在Scale-up與Scale-out兩種架構同步拉高頻寬需求，嘉澤在NPO、
既有高速銅傳輸與高功率連接方案的產品組合，將對應不同平台導入時程。

平台換代推升嘉澤營收，毛利率效益約遞延2–4季
 平台規格升級 → Socket針腳數增加 → ASP提高 → 伺服器營收增加 → 毛利金額提高
 平台發布後仍須經過客戶驗證、量產爬坡及 滲透率提升，效益通常不會在發布當季反映。

以上，圖形上約遞延2–4季。下一個平台為4Q26至2027年，預計4Q27毛利率回升。

營收走勢圖

研究員/張均壕 JUN_HAO1@uni-psg.com

研究部仍看好時碩未來營運，主要是考量液冷散熱零件業務隨著客
戶訂單逐步放量及出貨規模擴大 ，帶動 2H26 業績大幅提升。 此外 ，
航太事業成長前景仍佳，工業事業亦可望持續增長，明年展望依舊
正向。研究部預估時碩 2026 年 EPS 3.52 元(+162%YoY)，2027 年
EPS 4.63 元(+32%YoY)。以明年獲利為評價基礎，給予 20 倍本益
比，目標價 93 元。

重點摘要：

1. 受惠AI趨勢，液冷散熱訂單將帶動3Q26業績強勁增長： 
時碩3Q26合併營收上升至18.18億元(+24%QoQ，+43%YoY)，主
要是受惠AI資料中心液冷相關零組件的貢獻增加，令7、8、9
月單月營收持續創新高 。考量營收擴大， 規模效應顯現， 預期
時碩3Q26毛利率將提升至 24.5%，營益率提升至 8.8%，預估
3Q26稅後淨利1.38億元(+101%QoQ，+34%YoY)，預估3Q26EPS 
1.26元。

2. 液冷散熱產品進入爬坡期，4Q26營運淡季不淡： 
展望4Q26， 雖然年底通常為時碩汽車、工業業務的淡季， 但受
惠散熱零件仍處在出貨爬坡期，預期季度營收與獲利仍有望
再墊高。研究部預估時碩 4Q26合併營收18.85億元(+4%QoQ，
+55%YoY)，4Q26稅後淨利1.40億元(+1%QoQ，+113%YoY)， 預估
4Q26EPS 1.27元 。 全年度來看 ， 研究部預估時碩2026年合併營
收將提升至65.25億元(+31%YoY)，年度毛利率24.1%，營益率
7.1%，稅後淨利3.87億元(+161%YoY)，預估2026年EPS 3.52
元。

3. 航太業務維持成長動能，積極拓展新商機 
受惠於全球航太產業需求穩健及上游關鍵零組件產能吃緊，
時碩管理層持續看好航太業務前景 ，其中天陽航太營運成長
率預估可達15%～20%。 此外 ， 公司積極擴充泰國航太產能 ， 規
劃建置鋁合金及不鏽鋼生產線，以掌握全球航太供應鏈重組
及非紅供應鏈商機 ， 中長期目標為貢獻約10億元營收 。 除既有
航太業務外，時碩亦積極拓展人形機器人及半導體設備等新
興應用領域。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

在人形機器人方面， 時碩已投入諧波減速器及關節零組件開
發，並與國際及中國大陸主要廠商展開合作。半導體設備方
面，時碩持續開發相關精密零組件，未來將配合泰國新廠布
局 ， 爭取台灣客戶海外工廠的供應商機 。 整體而言 ， 隨著航太
業務穩健成長，以及人形機器人與半導體設備等新領域逐步
拓展 ， 將有助於公司建立更多元的營運成長動能 ， 降低對傳統
汽車產業的依賴。

4. 明年業績續升，預估2027年EPS 4.63元： 
展望2027年 ， 考量日系大廠的液冷散熱訂單將延續至明年 ， 而
且還有美系散熱大廠客戶訂單與磁浮壓縮機關鍵訂單等待發
酵 ， 加上航太業務仍具偏強增速 ， 預期時碩2027年業績將持續
提升。研究部預估時碩2027年合併營收74.20億元(+14%YoY)，
年度毛利率 25.0%， 預估2027年EPS將進一步提升至 4.63元。

## Document 177: 9 月会合以降に日銀を取り巻く状況が変わった印象

Source PDF: all report pdf/9 月会合以降に日銀を取り巻く状況が変わった印象.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

2026 年 10 月 7 日(水)

1/3 
9 月会合以降に日銀を取り巻く状況が変わった印象  
 経済調査部主席エコノミスト藤代宏一 
（℡：050-5474-6123）

・時事通信は10月６日付で「日銀が『基調的な物価上昇率』について、２％程度に到達したと
判断する方向で検討に入ることが５日、分かった」と報じた。記事内には10月29・30日開催
の金融政策決定会合で取りまとめる展望レポートで、そうした認識が記述されるとしてい
る。

・筆者はこの報道が現実になる可能性が高いと判断している。日銀は予てから基調的物価上昇
率が２％に到達する時期を「2026年度後半から2027年度」と予想してきたので（2026年度後
半にあたる）10月にそうした認識を示さないと、ハト派的な印象を与えてしまうことにな
る。

・既に日銀の政策態度は基調的物価上昇率を２％に「引き上げる」段階から、２％以上に「上
振れさせない」段階に移行している。局面が変化した理由としては、やはり原油高とそれに
対する企業の価格設定行動の積極化が大きいだろう。従来、原油高は企業収益を圧迫するこ
とで、賃上げを阻害し、個人消費の停滞に繋がってきた。そうした環境下で内需は拡大しな
いため、原油高は短期的にインフレ要因となっても、やや長い目でみるとデフレ的な色彩を
帯びていた。もっとも、2026年春先以降の原油高局面では、政府の手厚い支援もあり、企業
・日経平均株価は先行き12 ヶ月72,000 円程度で推移するだろう 
・USD/JPY は先行き12 ヶ月 155 円程度で推移するだろう 
・日銀は政策金利を12 月に 1.5％とした後、27 年末までに2.25％とするだろう 
・FED はFF 金利を、年内は4.0％で据え置くだろう

### Page 2

2/3 
は価格転嫁を積極的に進めた。企業物価指数は４月に前月比＋2.8％と即座に上昇し、前年比
伸び率は８月時点でも＋7.6％と高止まりしている。日銀短観（６月および９月調査）では１
年後の販売価格見通しが急伸した。こうした早期かつ大幅な価格転嫁の進展によって、企業
収益は傷みにくくなっている。このことは、名目賃金と名目消費が伸びやすい構図にあるこ
とを浮き彫りにしている。

・日銀が、基調的物価上昇率が２％に到達したとの認識を示すのであれば、それは利上げペー
スの加速を自ら正当化することになる。さすがに10月の利上げはビハインド・ザ・カーブに
陥ったことを認めたと市場参加者に受け止められる恐れがあるため、見送られると予想する
が、（前回利上げから約３ヶ月が経過する）12月会合では利上げが決定されるだろう。そう
なれば、３～４ヶ月に一度の利上げが標準ペースとなることが市場参加者に広く意識される
だろう。

・利上げを巡っては、経済指標に加えて「政府との距離感」を考慮する必要があり、予想は難
儀であった。しかしながら、ここ数週間で情勢は変化したように感じる。９月25日に片山さ
つき財務相が「官邸とも相談の上、私から初めて申し上げるが、先日の日米首脳会談では、
為替について、トランプ大統領から円安への懸念が示された」と発言したことを皮切りに、
同日には城内経済再生相が「アベノミクス的な意味でのリフレーション政策は、もうそうい
う局面は終わっている」とした。また10月５日に公表された９月30日の経済財政諮問会議の
議事要旨では、民間議員である若田部昌澄氏が「デフレではない状況になりつつある現状で
リフレ政策が終焉を迎えているというのは当然のことだ」との見解を示し、さらに９月の金
融政策決定会合で反対票を投じた佐藤綾野日銀政策委員は６日までに共同通信の単独インタ

### Page 3

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
3/3 
ビューに応じて「（政策金利を）段階的に調整していく方針には賛成している」とした。９
月18日会合における「主な意見」で内閣府の出席者（おそらく城内大臣）が利上げに消極的
と読める発言をしていたことと整合しないが、それは9月18日から25日の間に政府の態度が変
化した可能性が指摘できる。

・10月会合までに円安が進む、あるいは原油高等の要因によってＦｅｄが利上げに踏み切る事
態となれば、日銀も追随する可能性があるものの、そうした状況に至らなければ、10月は
「既往の利上げ効果を見極めたい」などとして利上げを見送るだろう。

・なお、これらに鑑みて、筆者は2027年７月の審議委員人事が中立ないしはタカ派的な人選に
なると予想する。高田委員と田村委員に考え方の近い人物に白羽の矢が立ち、リフレ派が送
り込まれることはないと考えている。利上げに積極的な２名の後任人事にリフレ派と目され
る人物が浮上した場合、金融市場では長期金利上昇や円安の反応が予想される。

## Document 190: [005380] 현대차(005380) 4분기 반등 기대현대차(005380) 4분기 반등 기대4분기 반등 기대 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005380] 현대차(005380) 4분기 반등 기대현대차(005380) 4분기 반등 기대4분기 반등 기대 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

현대차 (005380)

EARNINGS PREVIEW Company Analysis | 자동차/로봇 | 2026.10.16  
4분기 반등 기대

3Q26 Preview 
현대차는 3Q26E 매출액 42.4조원(-9.4% YoY, -13.9% QoQ), 영업이익 2.5조원(-
2.8% YoY, -13.5% QoQ, OPM 5.8%)을 기록, 컨센서스를 하회할 전망이다. 각 부문
별 영업이익은 자동차 1.7조원(-2.0% YoY, -14.9% QoQ, OPM 4.8%), 금융 5,501억
원(-4.5% YoY, -27.5% QoQ, OPM 6.9%)으로 예상한다. 
생산 정상화 및 신차 확대를 통한 수익성 개선 기대 
현대차는 글로벌 도매 판매 91.8만대(-11.5% 이하 YoY), 중국 제외 판매량은 90.9
만대(-9.6%)를 기록했다. 7~8월에 발생한 파업 영향으로 약 5.5만대 규모의 생산 차
질이 발생했다. 파업 영향으로 HEV 판매 증가율이 1%에 그치면서, HEV 판매 확대에 
따른 믹스 개선 효과도 시장 기대에 미치지 못했다.  
4분기부터는 3분기 파업 영향으로 지연된 생산량 회복 및 신차 확대가 본격화되면서, 
전분기 대비 매출 및 수익성 개선이 기대된다. 동사는 보급형 전기차 아이오닉3의 튀
르키예 생산을 9월부터 시작했으며, 이를 통해 유럽 시장 판매량은 점차 회복될 전망
이다. 또한, 1) HMGMA를 통한 HEV 미국 현지 생산 확대, 2) 제네시스 HEV 모델 순
차적 출시를 통해 내년부터 북미를 중심으로 믹스 개선이 본격화될 전망이다. 
투자의견은 Buy를 유지하고 목표주가는 520,000원으로 하향 
2027년 영업이익은 11.2조원(+10.2%)를 기록할 것으로 예상된다. 원화 약세 지속을 
반영해 이익 추정치를 하향하며, 이에 따라 목표주가를 하향한다. 다만, 현재 주가는 
12M Fwd P/E 기준 6.1배로 Legacy OEM 평균(7.7배)보다 낮은 수준이다. 1) RMAC 
성과 구체화, 2) RA(로봇 생산법인) 착공 등 Physical AI 사업이 구체화될 경우 밸류에
이션 할증이 가능할 전망이다. 
 Analyst 이병근 
bglee@ls-sec.co.kr 
Buy (유지)

## Document 203: [207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 파업 영향에도 견조한 실적, 4분기 성장 확대삼성바이오로직스(207940) 파업 영향에도 견조한 실적, 4분기_22fa3b02

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 파업 영향에도 견조한 실적, 4분기 성장 확대삼성바이오로직스(207940) 파업 영향에도 견조한 실적, 4분기_22fa3b02.pdf

(26.10.21)

제약업종

◼ 3Q26 파업 영향에도 컨센서스 상회 
◼ 4Q26 지연 물량 인식 및 신규 공장 기여로 실적 개선 전망 
◼ 투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지 
목표주가는 DCF 방식으로 산출(WACC 8.1%, 영구성장률 3.5% 적용). 기
존 공장의 높은 가동률을 기반으로 5공장 매출 기여 확대, 미국 생산거점 
확보, 신규 모달리티 진출을 통한 중장기 성장 지속 전망.

3Q26 Review: 파업 영향에도 컨센서스 상회  
3Q26 매출액 1조 4,903억원(+18.5% yoy, +12.8% qoq), 영업이익 6,401
억원(+1.1% yoy, +9.1% qoq), 영업이익률 42.9%(-7.5%p yoy)를 기록하며 
당사 추정치 및 시장 컨센서스 상회. 5월 1차 파업에 따른 매출 차질(약 
1,500억원) 에도 불구하고, 기존 1~4공장 Full 가동이 지속되며 견조한 실
적 달성. 5공장 PPQ 배치 생산 확대와 및 미국 록빌 공장 매출 인식도 외
형 성장에 기여. 다만, 신규 생산시설 초기 가동비와 제품 믹스 영향으로 
영업이익률은 전년 동기 대비 하락.

4Q26 매출액 1조 5,764억원(+22.6% yoy), 영업이익 7,075억원
(+34.0% yoy) 전망  
4Q26에는 2분기 파업으로 지연된 생산물량의 순차적인 매출 인식과 미국 
록빌 공장 매출 기여, 5공장 PPQ 배치 생산 확대로 실적 개선 예상, 동사
는 신규 생산시설의 매출 가시성 확보를 바탕으로 2026년 매출 성장률 
20%를 무난히 달성할 것으로 전망(연초 가이던스 15~20%).

신규 수주 회복과 포트폴리오 확장에 주목 
미국 의약품 관세정책 불확실성 완화로 지연됐던 수주 협상이 재개되며 신
규 수주 환경 개선 중. 3Q26 약 3억달러의 신규 수주를 확보했으며, 대형 
계약 협상도 구체화되고 있어 2027년 수주 회복 기대. 이는 5공장 가동률 
확대와 중장기 성장 가시성을 높이는 요인. 6공장은 ‘26년말~’27년초 착
공을 목표로 기존 증설 일정 유지. 
폴리펩타이드 그룹 인수는 1차 공개매수 응모율 87.88%를 기록하며 연내 
인수 완료 추진 중. 인수 완료 시 펩타이드 CDMO로 사업영역이 확대되며 
글로벌 생산거점 및 고객 기반 다변화 기대.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
• Recent revisions to US PCE inflation, UK productivity
and Canadian population estimates are reminders that
statistical measurement is imperfect. Revisions are both a
fact of life and, when they improve measurement, desirable.
• Australian data will also be revised over time, though

not necessarily in the same ways as these latest revisions
abroad. The ABS tends to favour quality over speed,
which reduces the need for early revisions as more data
become available. But revisions can still matter. Population
estimates implied in the initial releases of labour market and
GDP data are especially likely to be revised in the near term.
• Estimates of current trends can change as future data are
published. Shifting seasonal patterns are another source

of revision, especially for monthly CPI components with
short histories. Series that are derived from multiple other
imperfectly measured series are more prone to revision
than quantities that can be measured directly. All these
considerations point to a need for humility when using
recent data to make strong policy pronouncements.
On September 30, the Fed’s preferred measure of inflation,
PCE, was revised down. New methods and data sources were
applied to three components of services inflation (financial

advice, software and legal services), large enough to result in
downward revisions to the overall read on core PCE inflation.
These changes had been pre-announced in June and were
therefore not a surprise. The CPI measure of inflation was not
affected, nor should the revisions materially change your view
of US inflationary pressures.
Some revisions are more consequential for interpretation and
policy. A couple of weeks before the US PCE revision, it was

revealed that the UK Office for National Statistics was revising
down its estimates of average hours worked per worker. The
result was that measured productivity growth was nowhere
near as weak as had been widely believed. As my friend and
counterpart, TS Lombard’s Chief Economist Freya Beamish
commented on social media, “Oh well. Not like any major policy
decisions were taken on the basis of those numbers.” Quite.
Also in September, Statistics Canada revised up its estimates

of Canada’s population, taking the recent growth rates
from outright contraction to still-slow growth a little below
½%yr. The number of temporary residents in Canada is still
falling, but the revised data do a better job of tracking them,
especially those seeking an extension on their permit. (An alert
When reality gets revised
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
for customers who have seen the cross-country population
graph I often use at client events: the Canadian line now looks

different but still shows a sharp slowing.)
There is no fault to be attributed here. Revisions to key
statistics are a part of economic life. Sometimes the underlying
data arrive with a lag, perhaps because of reporting deadlines.
Sometimes the compiling agency improves its methods or
gains access to a better data source. The US and UK examples
above fall into this category. In the Canadian case, better
data and a more accurate definition of scope were involved.

All these revisions should be welcomed. They provide a more
accurate picture of the underlying reality.
Australia, revised
Revisions are a fact of life for Australian statistics, too. I am
still a little scarred by that unexpected –0.5%qtr GDP print
when the September quarter 2016 data were first released on
my first day as RBA Assistant Governor; the figure has since
been revised to 0%qtr and was followed by a big bounce-back
in the December quarter.

The recent revisions mentioned above are less likely to be
replicated in Australia, though. Australia’s CPI is designed such
that the headline (not seasonally adjusted) measure is never
revised. This is because it is used in many legal contracts.
If an error is detected, the ABS makes the adjustment in
subsequent months and quarters rather than revising the
history. Our labour force survey has a much higher response
rate than its UK equivalent – Australians are a compliant lot

when the ABS comes knocking – and measures actual hours
worked more reliably. And our population statistics already
handle the array of temporary resident visas with the Bureau
instead applying a standard 12-month-in-16-month residency
test to count as a resident for the population statistics rather
than using a visa-classification basis.
This last case illustrates Australia’s broader tendency to
favour quality over speed in official statistics. Our CPI and

national accounts data tend to be published a bit later than
peer countries’ data for the same reference period, and our
“Economic data are often revised. Sometimes
the revisions change the story; sometimes
they do not."

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
estimated resident population data is quite a bit lagged
relative to Canada’s (the 12-month-in-16 criteria taking over
a year to confirm). The trade-off is that observers of the
Australian economy must wait longer but can be less worried
about “flash” and “advance” releases of data that can be

quickly revised to tell a completely different story.
Revisions to data still can matter in Australia, and it helps
to be aware of the specific cases where it might occur. For
example, precisely because the population data are very
lagged, the population estimates underlying the initial
releases of the labour force survey and national accounts are
based on preliminary data and get revised later as a matter
of routine. This is why ratios such as the unemployment,

employment-to-population and participation rates provide a
better real-time gauge of labour market conditions than the
monthly change in employment. Similarly, GDP per capita can
be revised even when GDP is not. This type of revision is also
a more likely source of short-term changes in measured hours
worked in the Australian productivity data than the conceptual
change that shifted the UK data.
The usual reporting lags – and occasional correction of

reporting errors by the reporting entity – will always be a factor
in Australia just as it is in other countries. The annual national
accounts, due on 23 October, incorporate additional data, revise
the quarterly estimates and provide first estimates of important
quantities such as the capital stock. Just as importantly, though,
the annual accounts reconcile the different ways of measuring
GDP. In doing so, what we thought we knew about the recent

history of output growth can change.
Every purchase is also a sale, so conceptually, you should get the
same answer for final output regardless of whether you count
how much is produced, how much people spent on it, or how
much income people got for producing it. In practice, though,
the “production”, “expenditure” and “income” approaches do
not always line up until the ABS goes through a more thorough
reconciliation process in compiling the annual accounts.

When the future changes the past
Future data can also change our view of the past. Two good
examples are trend estimation and seasonal adjustment. Future
observations help reveal whether an apparent trend is stable.
As a result, real-time trend estimates can differ considerably
from those calculated with the benefit of later data.
Identifying stable seasonal patterns also requires a reasonable
run of data. Future observations affect whether an earlier

movement is judged to be normal seasonal variation. This
problem is more pronounced for newer series, where there is
not yet enough history to establish what “normal” looks like.
Consider, then, the monthly CPI. This relatively new measure has
only a short run of data with many individual components having
too little history to use standard seasonal adjustment methods.
As Westpac’s senior economist Justin Smirk has previously
highlighted, some components such as footwear are being

‘seasonally adjusted’ by applying Henderson trends, similar
to those used in the ‘trend’ measures of labour market and
national accounts data. For some other components, including
books, furniture, audio-visual and computing equipment and
children’s clothes, certain price falls – usually associated with
sales periods – are just arbitrarily removed from the seasonally
adjusted series. The index is held at its previous month’s level
but otherwise matches the non-seasonally adjusted series.

The seasonally adjusted measures of these CPI components will
inevitably be revised over time, and thus so could the monthly
trimmed mean estimates. This helps explain why the RBA has
said it would, for now, focus on the quarterly trimmed mean
based on the old seasonal factors. That measure will at least
not be buffeted by shifting measurement of seasonality in a
short history. If seasonal patterns are themselves also shifting,
the current underlying trend might look a bit different in a few

years, not just a smoother month-to-month profile.
The broader point here is to remember that economic statistics
are a lens on reality. Sometimes the lens is smudged, sometimes
the scope is off and sometimes what is being read as underlying
trends is wrong. Measures constructed from several imperfect
series – productivity from output and hours worked, or unit labour
costs from productivity and labour costs, or savings rates derived
from income and spending – are especially prone to revision

compared with concepts that can be measured more directly, like
a price. That is a strong argument for humility before making firm
policy pronouncements based on simple data comparisons.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: mulling the minutes
consistent with a labour market that is gradually normalising
than one entering a meaningful downturn.
The September FOMC minutes reinforced a distinctly hawkish
message, with participants noting they had "not seen

sufficient progress on lowering inflation in recent months" and
expressing concern that inflation risks remained "skewed to the
upside". Policymakers highlighted that both core services and
core goods inflation remained elevated, with some attributing
persistent goods price strength to the effects of the AI buildout
despite the sector's traditional disinflationary role. There was
also concern that, after more than five years of inflation above
target, elevated inflation could begin to influence inflation

expectations and wage- and price-setting behaviour. In
contrast, labour market conditions were generally viewed as
stable and "close to maximum employment". While a majority
noted the labour market had strengthened somewhat recently,
several participants highlighted unusually low dynamism,
characterised by low hiring, low layoffs, a low job-finding
rate and elevated long-term unemployment. Importantly,
participants generally judged that aggregate wage growth

was moderate and "consistent with inflation moving toward 2
percent", with several explicitly noting that "the labor market
was not currently a source of inflationary pressures".
While participants broadly expected AI-related investment to
boost productivity growth and potential output over time, several
warned that "the AI buildout could cause aggregate demand
to outpace aggregate supply over the medium term, putting
upward pressure on inflation". Consistent with this view, some

participants had raised their estimate of the neutral policy rate
and several judged the current policy stance to be "not restrictive
or only mildly restrictive". Against this backdrop, all participants
supported the September rate increase, and most assessed
that another increase in the federal funds rate would likely be
appropriate by year-end, although future decisions would remain
data dependent.
The September ISM Services PMI released this week painted

a similar picture of an economy that remains resilient but is
gradually losing momentum. The headline index eased to 54.9
from 55.4 in August, with the decline driven by a sharp fall in
business activity and a 9.4pt drop in new export orders, which
slipped into contractionary territory for the first time in eight
The only data release of note in Australia this week was
the October Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment Survey ,
which reported a 4.7% drop in the headline index. At

80.4, sentiment has tumbled back to deeply pessimistic
levels, similar to the post-pandemic cost-of-living shock
when readings were among the weakest in the survey’s
more than fifty-year history. Indeed, inflation and interest
rate pressures are once again clearly front of mind for
consumers, with both the view on ‘family finances vs a
year ago’ and ‘family finance next 12 months’ falling 8.0%
and 6.4% in the month, respectively. Moreover, the RBA’s

September rate hike looks to have had a significant impact –
sentiment tanked 20% between those surveyed before the
RBA decision and those surveyed afterwards.
Views on the economic outlook were relatively more resilient,
but they still weakened, with the one-year and five-year ahead
sub-indexes falling 1.6% and 0.4% respectively. Consumers
are becoming more ‘on edge’ about job security, albeit not
outright alarmed. The unemployment rate remains relatively

low versus history, although it has drifted up to a cycle high
of 4.6% as labour supply continues to outstrip demand. We
expect it to hold there in next week’s September Labour Force
Survey – see our preview for more detail. All told, with higher
interest rates, renewed inflation pressures and a softening
jobs market all weighing at once, households are entering the
final months of the year on the back foot.
Offshore, the focus was on US data.
Before turning to this week's releases, it is worth noting that

September non-farm payrolls came in softer than expected
across most metrics. Employment rose by only 29k (89k
expected), and there were sizeable downward revisions
to prior months, with a combined 60k removed from July
and August payrolls, leaving gains of just 31k and 29k
respectively. The details pointed to weaker hiring in leisure
& hospitality and education & health services industries,
although construction and manufacturing employment
continued to benefit from ongoing data-centre-related

investment. The unemployment rate edged up to 4.2%, with
softer employment occurring alongside a rise in labour force
participation to 61.8%. Meanwhile wage growth moderated
to 0.1%mth (3.0%yr). Taken together, the report suggests
labour demand is cooling, with the three-month average
pace of job creation slowing to around 50k. However, softer
payroll gains are not necessarily indicative of labour market
deterioration. Lower immigration and ageing demographics

have reduced the pace of employment growth needed to keep
the unemployment rate stable. As such, the report is more
Sian Fenner, Head of Business and Industry Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist
“A key focus was the interaction between
AI investment, productive capacity and
inflation."

months. Labour market conditions improved modestly with
the employment index moving to broadly neutral territory
50.1 after two months below 50, supported by elevated
backlogs and still-solid demand. Respondent commentary
illustrated the mixed nature of labour demand, with some

firms reporting they were "filling positions vacated due to
promotions or retirements", while others cited "restructuring
due to efficiencies gained using AI tools". Cost pressures also
remained elevated, with the prices paid index rising to 74.0,
its highest level since July 2022 and around 18pts above its
historical average, while fuel costs, tariffs and supply chain
constraints remained the most frequently cited concerns.
Overall, the report suggests services activity remains

consistent with ongoing expansion, albeit at a slower pace,
while labour demand and pricing pressures remain firm.
Overall, the data supports our view that the FOMC will move
to a broadly neutral policy stance by the end of the year with
one more rate hike, although the case for a back-to-back move
in October has become less clear cut.

### Page 6

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
Fuel, housing and the bumpy road ahead
Higher fuel costs, a weaker exchange rate, and a softer
housing market have prompted a refresh of our near-term
forecasts. Inflation looks set to finish 2026 above 4%, while
growth could lose a little momentum into year-end. Even so,

we continue to expect the RBNZ to pause in October before
lifting the OCR to 3% in December.
New Zealand’s recovery is still moving forward, but the road
has become rougher. Since our last forecast update in August,
global oil and refined-fuel prices have climbed sharply, the
New Zealand dollar has fallen, and longer-term interest rates
have moved higher. At home, the housing market has remained
weaker than we anticipated. None of those developments,

on their own, are enough to derail the recovery. Together,
however, they point to a tougher finish to 2026 for households
and businesses. We have therefore revisited our near-term
inflation, housing and growth forecasts. The broad story
remains one of continued economic expansion and gradually
improving labour demand, but with a more pronounced
inflation spike and slightly less momentum in consumer
spending. Our OCR call is unchanged: a pause at the October

review, followed by a 25 basis point increase to 3% at the
December Monetary Policy Statement.
A temporary inflation reprieve
We continue to think that consumer prices rose 0.7% in the
September quarter. That would lower annual inflation from
4.1% in June to 3.7% in September, broadly consistent with
the monthly price updates published so far. The final monthly
release for the quarter is due on 16 October and will help firm up
that estimate. But any easing in the annual rate is likely to prove

short-lived. We now expect prices to rise 1.1% in the December
quarter, lifting annual inflation to a three-year high of 4.2% by
year-end. That is half a percentage point above our previous
projection and above the RBNZ’s September forecast of 3.9%.
The reason is easy to see at the petrol pump. Higher global
fuel prices have been amplified by a more than 4% fall in
the New Zealand dollar since the start of September. Local
petrol prices have risen by around 50 cents a litre over the

past month, with the nationwide average price of 91 unleaded
reaching a record $3.55 a litre this week. RBNZ Governor
Breman acknowledged a couple of weeks ago that higher
oil prices have tilted the risks around the near-term inflation
outlook to the upside. Importantly, however, the RBNZ remains
focused on the medium-term inflation outlook rather than
trying to offset every movement in volatile fuel prices.
For now, we have left our quarterly inflation forecasts for 2027

unchanged. Even so, the higher starting point at the end of this
year means annual inflation is likely to remain firm through much
of 2027. Our updated profile has inflation at 3.7% in March
2027, 2.6% in June, 2.5% in September and 1.7% in December.
That is broadly similar to the RBNZ’s path through the first three
quarters, although our forecast falls more quickly late in the
year. We will take a fuller look at the 2027 inflation and growth
outlook next week alongside the monthly Market Outlook.

Housing fails to find its feet
The housing market has also delivered a disappointment. We
had expected prices to stabilise through the second half of
2026, but sales volumes have continued to weaken, and the
median time required to sell a property has lengthened to 48
days. Average prices have kept edging lower, despite firmer
conditions in parts of the South Island with greater exposure
to the strong export economy. The latest indicators do not
suggest that a decisive turn is close. We have therefore revised

our forecast for national house price growth in 2026 from a
0.6% rise to a 1.4% decline. That incorporates quarterly falls
of 0.5% in September and 0.8% in December.
The headwinds are familiar: the labour market remains soft,
mortgage rates are higher and employment growth is still
moderate. Those forces are likely to keep the market subdued
for some time, with risks tilted towards another weak year
in 2027. Changes to the taxation of capital gains on investor

housing following the 2026 General Election may add another
potential drag.
Kelly Eckhold
Chief Economist NZ

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
7
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Regional outcomes will continue to differ. Tourism- and
agriculture-focused areas should benefit from stronger income
and employment growth, supported by robust commodity export
prices. We have lifted our 2026/27 Fonterra Milk Price forecast to
$9.75/kg and set an opening forecast of $9.50/kg for 2027/28. In

contrast, homeowners in the major North Island cities may have to
wait longer for the wealth boost that comes with rising property
values. Data released this week shows that household wealth
continues to be dragged down by declining housing values.
A little less momentum
The weaker housing backdrop matters for the wider economy.
When petrol absorbs more of the household budget and home
values are drifting lower, consumers tend to become more
cautious. That combination is likely to crimp discretionary

spending in the run-up to Christmas and could take some
steam out of the construction recovery. That’s even though our
own card spending data for September  showed resilience in
consumer spending. Recent business and consumer confidence
surveys have nevertheless remained resilient despite the rise
in energy prices and long-term interest rates. Our short-term
growth forecasts therefore remain broadly intact, but we have
trimmed our December quarter consumer spending projection

and now expect GDP growth of 2.5% over 2026. That is still
a solid result, although the balance of risks has shifted to
the downside.
There are several moving parts to watch. Sustained high fuel
prices would squeeze household purchasing power more than
we have allowed for. The sharp lift in long-term interest rates
may also weigh on activity through 2027, even if shorter-
term borrowing costs evolve as expected. The election result
introduces two-sided risks that are difficult to quantify before

the shape and timing of policy changes are known. Against that,
strong agricultural incomes and signs of improving employment
growth should provide a buffer. We have not changed our 2026
labour market forecasts. Hiring appears to be strengthening
gradually, but it will take time before that improvement
translates into a noticeable fall in the unemployment rate.
The RBNZ should still pause before Christmas
Despite the higher near-term inflation track, we are not

changing our OCR forecast. Since the July Monetary Policy
Review, our view has been that the RBNZ would slow the pace
of increases as the OCR approached the Bank’s estimate of a
neutral 3%. The September meeting clearly signalled a pause
in October and one further rise in December. We continue to
regard that as the most likely path. The latest developments
raise headline inflation but, so far, there is little evidence that
inflation expectations are moving higher or that the fuel shock

is generating broader, persistent price pressure. At the same
time, growth risks warrant some caution.
A pause in October would give the Monetary Policy Committee
time to assess the cumulative effect of the increases already
delivered and to refine its strategy for 2027. Financial
conditions have also tightened from an unexpected source:
longer-term interest rates have risen markedly over the past
six weeks. That increase has at least offset the easing impulse

from the lower exchange rate, even though the weaker
currency is adding to imported inflation in the near term. We
still think the RBNZ is likely to have more work to do in 2027,
but there is plenty of data to come before that judgement
needs to be made.
For now, the best description of the outlook is resilient but
less comfortable. Export incomes remain strong, activity is
expanding and the labour market is beginning to improve. But
households face an awkward combination of record petrol

prices, soft home values and higher borrowing costs. That
mix points to a bumpy end to the year: inflation will be hotter,
spending growth a little cooler and the recovery more uneven
across regions. The RBNZ can afford to wait in October, but if
the economy develops broadly as we expect, a further hike to
3% in December will still be needed.

### Page 8

AUS: Sep Labour Force – Employment Change (000s)
Oct 15, Last: 39.5, Westpac f/c: 20 
Market f/c: 20, Range: flat to 35
Employment surprised to the upside in August with a 39k gain. 
Looking through the monthly swings, while momentum has 
improved over recent months, employment growth has largely 
continued to bump around below its historic average and the 
current pace of working age population growth.
Our preview provides a deeper look into the various gauges 
of labour demand – the LFS suggests employment growth 
is sluggish but broader measures in the Q2 Labour Account 
showed a stronger narrative. Meanwhile job ads and business 
surveys are also pulling in different directions.
For September, we have pencilled in a lift in employment of 
+20k, which would see annual growth hold in recent ranges.
AUS: Sep Labour Force – Unemployment Rate (%)
Oct 15, Last: 4.6, Westpac f/c: 4.6 
Market f/c: 4.6, Range: 4.5 to 4.7
The participation rate jumped to 67.1% in August, just under 
the 115-year high recorded in January 2025. With labour 
supply once again outstripping demand, the unemployment 
rate rose from 4.5% to 4.6% – the highest since in late-2021, 
close to rounding up to 4.7% (4.646% at three decimal places).
A softer economy and sub-par employment growth typically 
discourages some people to search for work, but cost-of-living 
pressures and interest rate rises are more than outweighing this 
effect, encouraging more people into the labour market.
With the participation rate expected to pare back to 67.0%, we 
think the unemployment rate will hold steady at 4.6%. Please 
see our preview for more detail.

### Page 9

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.9
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
NZ: Sep REINZ House Sales and Prices (%ann)
Oct 14, Sales Last: –13.0, Prices Last: –0.9
The housing market has remained soft in recent months,
with sales falling to a two-year low and prices edging lower.
Mortgage rates have risen as the RBNZ has initiated a new

tightening cycle, and a growing supply of unsold homes on the
market means that buyers are under no pressure to bid prices
higher. Conditions remain stronger in the south, with prices
up on last year in Canterbury, Otago and Southland, while
Auckland and Wellington are the weakest markets.
NZ: Sep Retail Card Spending (%mth)
Oct 16, Last: –0.9, Westpac f/c: 0.8
We’re forecasting a 0.8% rise in retail spending in September,
reversing the 0.9% fall seen in the previous month. In part, the

forecast rise in spending is due to the sharp increases in petrol
prices in recent weeks. Those higher fuel costs are weighing on
spending in other categories like hospitality. Nevertheless, we
still expect a 0.5% rise in core (ex-fuel) spending. Stats NZ’s
monthly retail spending figures have been very choppy for the
past two years, and we’re likely to see a bounce following last
month’s unexpected falls in categories like furnishings.
NZ: Sep Selected Consumer Prices (%mth)

Oct 16
This will be the last monthly update ahead of the full quarterly
CPI release on 22 October. We’re currently forecasting a 0.7%
quarterly rise in the CPI (+3.7%yr) and will be watching the
detail of the Selected Prices update to pin down that forecast.
In terms of the big categories, we’re forecasting a roughly 6%
rise in petrol costs, along with a 0.7% rise in food prices. That’s
balanced against continued softness in housing rents (which
are the single largest component of the CPI). The key sources

of risk for our forecast are the volatile travel categories, most
of which recorded sizeable drops last month that we expect to
partially reverse in September.

◼

 : 
the government debt burden 
 is moderate and 
 will remain

deteriorate
 . The fiscal deficit could exceed 3% of GDP in 2026 due to support measures in response to the energy shock (estimated at 
less than 1% of GDP in H1 2026), but this will not endanger public
 -
 debt dynamics. Government debt should fall again in 2027 thanks 
to lower deficits and fast GDP growth. Debt was only 30% of GDP in 2025 (vs. 41% in 2019) and interest payments consumed less  
than 5% of fiscal revenue (vs. 7.2% in 2019). The government cover
 s its funding needs mostly in the local bond market, which is 
deepening gradually and not 
 highly 
 exposed to foreign investors. Government debt in foreign currency declined to about 25% of total 
debt in 2025 (vs. 38% in 2019); this is still substantial, but almost all this FC debt 
 is made up  
 of concessional loans from official 
creditors. On the negative front, high credit risks in the state corporate and bank sectors 
 are 
 very large contingent risks for the 
sovereign. Moreover, public investment needs related to climate change and infrastructure development will increase in the 
 medium 
term
.

◼ 
 Systemic credit risk
 : 
Domestic credit to the private sector is too large and still rising (141% of GDP in 2025 vs. 108% in 2019) while 
the banking sector remains poorly capitali
 s
ed. Credit risks and resource misallocation have long been exacerbated by opacity and 
supervision flaws, as well as the use of high credit growth targets to support economic growth (+15% in 2026). In 2022
 –
2023, banks 
faced severe funding stress and asset
 -
 quality deterioration resulting from the property and bond market crisis. The situation has 
recovered gradually since then. In the 
 short term
 , credit risks may increase amid higher energy costs and if  
there is  
a correction in 
the tech cycle. Some banks, especially small private ones, also remain exposed to VND funding risk (whereas banks do not rely  
much 
on 
 foreign
-
 currency 
 financing). Positively, reforms, including stricter capital
 -
 adequacy requirements, continue to be introduced 
gradually 
 in order 
 to improve banks’ creditworthiness (
 the 
 SBV plans to test the removal of credit quotas for the soundest banks 
from 2026).

## Document 316: Irrational Exuberance In S&P 500 FEMO_

Source PDF: all report pdf/Irrational Exuberance In S&P 500 FEMO_.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: Irrational Exuberance In S&P 500 FEMO?
Date:    6 hours ago
Irrational Exuberance In S&P 500 FEMO?

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
Irrational Exuberance In S&P
500 FEMO?
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 21 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Chris Geirman / Unsplash
Are analysts' earnings expectations for the S&P 500 irrationally
exuberant? So far this year, FEMO (fabulous earnings momentum, as
measured by actual earnings per share) has been soaring, while FOMO
(fear of missing out, as reflected in P/E multiples) has been mostly
retreating. Much of that earnings optimism is concentrated in the S&P 500
Information Technology sector, particularly the Semiconductors industry,

### Page 3

where the AI investment boom has fueled extraordinary demand, pricing
power, and profit margins. But can these conditions persist? Let's take a
closer look:
(1) FEMO overwhelming FOMO. The S&P 500 is up 13.3% ytd, despite a
13.5% contraction in its forward P/E. That's because FEMO has
overwhelmed waning FOMO, with forward earnings jumping 31.0% since
the start of the year (chart).
The divergence is even more striking in Information Technology. The

sector's forward earnings has soared 65.5% ytd, while its forward P/E has
plunged 25.9%. The S&P 500 IT stock price index has risen 22.6% ytd,
powered by FEMO (chart).

### Page 4

Industry analysts remain remarkably bullish on the S&P 500's
fundamentals. Forward revenues is up 15.7% y/y, while forward earnings
is up 39.0% (chart).
(2) Semiconductors driving FEMO. Much of that optimism originates in
the IT sector, where forward revenues and earnings are up 57.2% and
91.4% y/y, respectively (chart).
Digging deeper reveals that the sector's extraordinary FEMO originates
largely in the Semiconductors industry, where forward revenues and

earnings are up 121.7% and 176.0% y/y, respectively (chart).

### Page 5

Indeed, semiconductors now account for a record 55.0% of the IT sector's
forward earnings and 40.9% of its market capitalization (chart).
The growing importance of semiconductors extends to the broader
market. Semiconductors now account for a record 20.4% of the S&P 500’s
forward earnings and 15.8% of its market capitalization (chart).

### Page 6

That makes the industry's earnings trajectory increasingly important for
the entire index. Analysts expect Semiconductor revenues and earnings to
grow 61.3% and 74.0% over the next year, respectively. More remarkably,
they expect earnings growth to average 66.1% annually over the next five
years.
These extraordinary expectations help explain why analysts project IT
sector earnings growth of 44.8% over the next year and 42.0% annually
over the next five years (chart).

In turn, analysts expect S&P 500 earnings to grow 19.0% over the next
year and an average of 26.1% annually over the next five years, the latter
approaching a record high.

### Page 7

(3) Pricing power fueling Semiconductors' FEMO. To determine
whether S&P 500 FEMO shows irrational exuberance, we need to assess
whether Semiconductor FEMO does so. Much of the industry's
extraordinary earnings growth reflects unprecedented profit margin
expansion. The industry’s forward profit margin nearly doubled from the
2023 lows to a record 51.8% during the week of October 20 (chart).
The explanation is straightforward: Demand for chips has surged as

hyperscalers and neocloud providers have raced to build AI infrastructure,
while supply is capacity constrained. The resulting pricing pressures are
clearly evident in the PPI for electronic components & accessories (up
26.8% y/y through September) and the import price index for computer &
electronic product manufacturing (up 12.4% y/y through September)
(chart).

### Page 8

(4) Will Semiconductors' pricing power persist? That's the critical
question for assessing whether Semiconductor FEMO is irrationally
exuberant. Investors already seem skeptical. The Semiconductor
industry's forward P/E has plunged to just 14.2 despite surging earnings
expectations (chart). In other words, unlike the tech bubble of 1999, any
irrational exuberance in FEMO has been mostly offset by depressed
FOMO!
The goods news is that demand for chips should remain strong. The nine

largest cloud service providers are expected to spend $957 billion on
capex this year, up 106% y/y, and more than $1.5 trillion in 2027. But
supply is starting to catch up. Global semiconductor manufacturing
capacity is expected to expand 5% this year and another 6% in 2027,
potentially easing shortages and price pressures.
(5) Irrational earnings exuberance? We suspect there is some irrational
exuberance in Semiconductor FEMO, particularly in analysts' consensus

long-term earnings growth expectations. Today's demand-supply
imbalance supports extraordinary profits, but sustaining current pricing
power and margins over a longer time horizon seems optimistic as
capacity expands. With Semiconductors accounting for more than one-
fifth of S&P 500 forward earnings, that exuberance is reflected in the
broader index.
Nevertheless, FEMO remains robust beyond Semiconductors. Analysts
expect Q3 earnings growth of 30.7% y/y, with positive growth across all 11

sectors (chart).

### Page 9

Our diffusion indexes also show that an increasing share of S&P 500
companies is experiencing rising forward revenues and earnings (chart).
Combined with expectations that semiconductor demand will continue to
outpace supply, at least over the near term, this suggests that FEMO
remains fundamentally rational.
💡

leave comments or questions at the end of our QuickTakes, and it
may be featured in the next "Just Ask Dr. Ed!"

### Page 10

clause.

## Document 399: Westpac Nowcast Q3 2026_ Second Estimate (2026-10-25)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Westpac Nowcast Q3 2026_ Second Estimate (2026-10-25).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-25

Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
1
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
WESTPAC NOWCAST Q3
2026: SECOND ESTIMATE
Growth falters through the quarter
16 October 2026
Key points
• The second estimate from Westpac-Now
points to GDP growth of around +0.5%qtr
in Q3 2026 and 2.1%yr. This is slightly lower
than the first estimate of +0.6%qtr. This

still represents a slight step up in growth
from Q2 2026 but masks some loss of
momentum over the course of the quarter.
• Our Monthly Activity Index deteriorated
noticeably in August and September.
Weakness has become increasingly
broad-based, with labour and business
condition indicators among the
largest drags and relatively few areas
providing a meaningful offset.
• The economy is coming under increasing
pressure as the cumulative impact of
higher interest rates combines with

renewed disruptions in global energy
markets and a deepening housing
downturn. With another rate hike likely in
November, these pressures are expected
to build further heading into 2027.
• Overall, the economy is becoming
increasingly reliant on the upswing in
business investment to support growth.
This narrow base of growth has left the
balance of risks less favourable. Our central
case remains one of slowing rather than
stalling. Westpac-Now’s current model

dynamics point to growth moderating
to around +0.4%qtr in Q4 2026. Our
formal forecasts have growth slowing
to a softer +0.2%qtr pace (see here).
• Our final Westpac-Now estimate for Q3
2026 will be published on 20 November.

2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
In November 2025, we introduced Westpac-Now (see here),
a continuously-updated measure of current activity in the
Australian economy. Westpac-Now draws on more than
60 high frequency economic and financial variables, using
advanced statistical techniques to provide a timely pulse

check on economic momentum. It is the first Australian
model to incorporate internal banking data, enhancing its
predictive power.
This note provides the second estimate of GDP growth for the
September quarter 2026, using domestic and international
data released up to and including the September Labour Force
Survey (released 15 October 2026). Updates are published
monthly (see Appendix A for the release schedule).
Westpac Monthly Activity Index: activity falters as

headwinds mount
After showing tentative signs of improvement through the
second half of 2025, the Westpac Monthly Activity Index has
weakened markedly over 2026. The Index moved back into
negative territory in February and has declined further since.
While there were some signs of stabilisation around mid-year,
this proved to be short lived with the Index falling to –0.88 in
September, outside of the pandemic period, the weakest read
since 2013.
The economy is coming under pressure from multiple

fronts, with key headwinds coming from higher interest
rates, renewed disruptions to global energy markets and a
deepening in the housing downturn. Among households,
spending held up surprisingly well through the first half of the
year despite historically weak sentiment, but more recently
Growth momentum on shaky ground
spending outside of fuel has started to cool (see here). At
the same time, the labour market has continued to soften,
with unemployment reaching a cycle high of 4.7% (see here).

Meanwhile, the business sector is also showing signs of
softening, with sentiment deteriorating and conditions holding
at weak levels (see here). Business credit growth remains
a notable exception, although even here there are tentative
signs of easing (see here).
This deterioration is particularly evident in our heatmap.
Negative contributions have become both broader and more
pronounced through 2026, with the weakness intensifying
in August and September. Labour and business condition

indicators are now the largest sources of weakness, while
spending and confidence have also deteriorated materially.
Credit and capacity utilisation indicators have been
comparatively less negative, but there are currently few areas
providing a meaningful offset to the broader softening.
Our indicator heatmap decomposes changes in the Monthly
Activity Index into group-level contributions, allowing us to
assess which components are driving movements in activity.

For ease of interpretation, indicators are grouped into broad
categories (see the Appendix for further details). For example,
the labour market category includes both official ABS Labour
Force Survey outcomes and timelier survey-based measures of
labour demand and job advertisements.
Westpac-Now: Resilient growth outlook slowing not
stalling
Recall that Westpac-Now is a refined indicator that combines
our Monthly Activity Index with advanced econometric

techniques to estimate GDP growth. As previously noted,
the fit is not expected to be exact. Measured GDP includes
Neha Sharma
Economist

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
components that are not well captured by high-frequency
activity indicators, such as imputed rents for owner-occupiers,
housing-related ownership transfer costs, public and business
inventories, and depreciation.
To bridge monthly and quarterly data, we use a MIDAS

framework, allowing us to combine mixed-frequency inputs
and incorporate partial information for the current quarter
when producing GDP forecasts.
Based on current model estimates, Westpac-Now’s second
estimate for Q3 2026 points to GDP growth of 0.5%qtr
(with a 70% confidence interval of –0.1%qtr to 1.0%qtr)
and 2.1%yr, representing a slight downgrade from the first
estimate of +0.6%qtr but still a step up from the 0.4%qtr
(2.1%yr) outcome recorded in Q2 2026. While aggregate

growth appears to have held up through Q3, the underlying
picture became softer as the quarter progressed. At the
second decimal place the estimate is 0.46%qtr, leaving it
close to rounding down to 0.4%qtr.
With several headwinds now bearing on the economy
simultaneously, there is a growing risk that the slowdown in
activity becomes more pronounced than currently anticipated.
At the same time, the strength of the business investment
upswing provides an important counterweight that should

help cushion the broader slowdown.
Looking ahead, model dynamics suggest growth will tick
down to 0.4%qtr in Q4 2026 before picking up in Q1 2027.
However, these projections remain much more tentative,
given their reliance on projected inputs rather than realised
data. In addition, heightened uncertainty, particularly around
the renewed tensions in the Middle East conflict, means risks
around these forward estimates are larger than usual.
September Quarter data: a softer end to the quarter

Data released in the first month of the September quarter
indicated that the economy has picked up momentum relative
to the previous quarter, with our first nowcast estimating Q3
growth at 0.6%qtr, in line with the 0.6%qtr “pure forecast”
based solely on data available up to June 2026.
However, the flow of data since then shows downside risks to
growth have begun to materialise. Incorporating the August,
followed by the September (to date), data both yielded

nowcasts of 0.5%qtr (with a slight softening at the second
decimal place). This contrasts with Q2 2026, when weakness
emerged early in the quarter, in April, before subsequent data
pointed to stabilisation.
Note that monthly activity estimates are more uncertain
than normal when inflation is shifting. Faster price growth
can cause nominal indicators, such as consumer spending,
to rise more strongly even though ‘real’, inflation-adjusted
spending may be softening (and vice versa). The inclusion

of ‘real’ indicators, such as employment and banking
transaction volumes, helps mitigate some – but not all – of this
uncertainty.
Bottom line:
Westpac-Now points to GDP growth of around 0.5%qtr
(2.1%yr) in Q3 2026, suggesting aggregate growth held up
reasonably well through the quarter, although the flow of
data points to softer momentum as the quarter progressed.
The substantial upswing in business investment remains
an important source of support, helping to offset mounting

weakness elsewhere in the economy.
Looking ahead, the accumulation of headwinds is expected to
see growth slow further towards year-end, with Westpac-Now
currently pointing to growth of around 0.4%qtr in Q4 2026.
Downside risks around the outlook have increased, but for now
our central case remains one of slower growth rather than a
material downturn.

Going forward, Westpac Now will be published on the Friday following each ABS Labour Force Survey (LFS) release, at 11:30am AEDT/AEST (Canberra time).
Each release incorporates the latest labour market data alongside other high frequency indicators and provides an updated nowcast for quarterly GDP growth.

## Document 407: “It’s the economy, stupid.” K字化が揺らす米国中間選挙

Source PDF: all report pdf/“It’s the economy, stupid.” K字化が揺らす米国中間選挙.pdf
Report source: DIR
Date: 2026-10-22
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

米国

株式会社大和総研 丸の内オフィス 〒100-6756 東京都千代田区丸の内一丁目 9 番 1 号 グラントウキョウ ノースタワー 
このレポートは投資勧誘を意図して提供するものではありません。このレポートの掲載情報は信頼できると考えられる情報源から作成しておりますが、その正確性、完全
性を保証するものではありません。また、記載された意見や予測等は作成時点のものであり今後予告なく変更されることがあります。㈱大和総研と大和証券㈱は、㈱大和

2026 年 10 月 22 日 全9 頁

“It’s the economy, stupid.”  
K 字化が揺らす米国中間選挙 
中間選挙では、上下院で民主党が僅差で多数派になると予想

経済調査部 主任研究員 矢作 大祐

[要約] 
 2026 年11 月3 日の米中間選挙では、景気指標の底堅さと有権者の生活実感との乖離が
選挙結果を左右しうる。1992 年大統領選挙では、“It’s the economy, stupid.”を掲げ
た民主党のクリントン氏がブッシュ（父）氏に勝利した。米国の選挙では、有権者の
景気・物価に対する評価が選挙結果を予想する上でも重要になるといえよう。 
 足元の米国経済は実質GDP 成長率が前年比＋2％強と底堅く推移する一方、成長を牽引
するのは AI 関連投資や高所得層の消費に偏る。低中所得層では実質賃金の低下、貯蓄
余力の縮小、クレジットカードローン延滞率の上昇が重なり、景気拡大の恩恵を感じ
にくい「K 字経済（良し悪しが二分化する状態）」が鮮明になっている。 
 こうした低中所得層を中心とした生活苦は、トランプ大統領の支持率や消費者マイン
ドを押し下げ、与党共和党への逆風となっている。中間選挙のメインシナリオは、民
主党が上下院で僅差の多数派を獲得するというものだ。下院では民主党による多数派
奪還の公算が大きく、上院でも激戦州の結果次第で民主党が優位に立つとみる。ただ
し、民主党内の急進左派候補が無党派層を取り込めないリスクもあり、圧勝は見込み
にくい。 
 注目点は議席数だけでなく、民主党の「勝ち方」と共和党の「負け方」となる。各州
の結果は、2028 年大統領選挙を見据えた両党の主導権争いや、AI 規制・課税を含む経
済政策にも影響を及ぼしう る。そして、今回の中間選挙で民主党が躍進すれば、実質
GDP 成長率などのヘッドラインの数字だけでは捉えられない、米国社会の不満と経済
の脆弱性が浮き彫りになるだろう。

### Page 2

1

2 / 9

経済情勢が左右する米中間選挙の結果 
2026 年 11 月 3 日に実施される米国中間選挙が近づいている。市場参加者の関心は、選挙後
の議会構成やトランプ政権の政策運営に集まっている。しかし、中間選挙を考える上で重要な
のは、足元の米国経済の実態を把握することだ。1992 年の大統領選挙で、民主党のビル・クリ
ントン氏は“It’s the economy, stupid.”（ 「重要なのは経済だ、愚か者」 ）という有名なスロ
ーガンを掲げ、当時の大統領であったブッシュ（父）氏に勝利した。景気や物価に対する有権
者の評価が選挙結果を左右するということは、米国における選挙の基本的な特徴といえる。 
もっとも、現在の米国経済は「好調」か「不調」か、という単純な二択では捉えにくい。経
済全体は底堅い一方、その内側では、成長の恩恵を受ける分野・主体と、恩恵を実感しにくい
分野・主体の差が広がっている。本稿では、こうした米国経済の K 字化（良し悪しが二分化す
る状態）の進展がトランプ大統領の支持率と中間選挙にどのような影響を及ぼしているのかを
整理し、中間選挙の注目点や見通しを提示する。

ヘッドラインは堅調、実態は K 字経済 
米国経済全体を俯瞰すると、一見して大きな問題は見当たらない。2026年10 月のIMF「世界
経済見通し」でも、米国は先進国の中で高い成長率を維持している国の一つと評価されている。
2026 年の実質 GDP 成長率は 4-6 月期までは前年比＋2％強と、おおむね潜在成長率並みのペー
スで推移している。このため、米国経済は堅調というのが一般的な見方だ。しかし、実質 GDP
の内訳を見ると様相は大きく異なる。米国経済の二枚看板である設備投資と個人消費を比較す
ると、設備投資が堅調である一方、個人消費はやや冴えない（図表1 左図） 。経済全体として成
長が続いていても、その構成項目は同じ方向を向いていない。好調な設備投資と軟調な個人消
費が併存する、いわゆるK 字経済の様相を呈している。 
また、K 字化は多層化している。設備投資に関してもAI 投資が積極化し、情報通信機器やソ
フトウェアなどのIT 関連投資は大幅なプラスとなっている一方、それ以外の投資はマイナス圏
に沈んでいる（図表 1 右図）。米国では、こうした状況を“Non-AI Recession”（「AI 以外の景
気後退」）と呼ぶ向きもある。AI 関連分野が投資全体を押し上げる裏側で、その他の産業には
停滞感が残っている。 
K 字化は企業部門だけの現象ではなく、個人消費も同様だ。実質小売販売額を所得階層別に
見ると、高所得層が相対的に高い伸びを維持している一方、低中所得層の伸びは弱い（図表 2
左図）。高所得層の消費が強い第一の理由 として、株高による資産効果 が挙げられる 。S&P500
が最高値を更新する中、株式や投資信託などのリスク資産を多く保有する家計ほど、資産価値
の上昇を通じて消費余力が高まりやすい。株・投信の保有残高に関する高所得層（上位20％）
対低中所得層（それ以外）の倍率は 7 倍程度かつ、上昇基調にあり、株高の恩恵は高所得層に
集中している（図表 2 右図）。また、第二の理由として、2025 年 7 月に成立した「トランプ減

### Page 3

1

3 / 9

税 2.0」もK 字化を助長していると考えられる1。いわゆるトランプ減税2.0 による所得階層別
の家計所得への影響に関しては、高所得層の改善幅が大きい一方、低中所得層への恩恵は相対
的に小さい。株高と減税が同時に高所得層の消費余力を支えることで、家計における K 字化が
さらに進みやすい構造となっている。

低中所得層は生活苦に直面し、与党共和党は逆風にさらされる 
株高や減税の恩恵が限られていても、賃金が十分に増えれば家計の不満は和らぐ。しかし、
足元では賃金の伸びが鈍化している 。物価上昇に加え、名目賃金の伸び自体も減速しており
（図表 3 左図） 、実質賃金は前年比でマイナスとなっている。労働所得への依存度が高い低中所
得層にとって、暮らし向きは改善しにくい。家計はこれまで、貯蓄に回す資金を抑えることで
消費を維持してきた。しかし、実質可処分所得が伸び悩む中で貯蓄率はすでに低水準にあり、
さらに貯蓄を削る余地は限られる。低中所得層のように多額の金融資産を保有していない家計
ほど、賃金の伸び悩みがそのまま消費余力の低下につながりやすい（図表3 右図）。 
図表 3 名目賃金と実質賃金、貯蓄率とクレジットカードローン延滞率

（出所）BLS、BEA、NY 連銀、Haver Analytics より大和総研作成

加えて、クレジットカードローンの延滞率はリーマン・ショック直後の水準に近づいている。
2022 年から 2023 年に貯蓄率が大幅に低下したこともあったが、当時はコロナ禍における超過
貯蓄があり、クレジットカードローン延滞率は低水準だった。貯蓄率が低下し、借り入れ状況
も悪化している中で、家計部門の消費余力は黄色信号がともっているといえよう。仮に株価が
調整し、高所得層の資産効果まで弱まれば、消費全体の下振れリスクは大きくなりかねない。 
こうした生活苦は政治にも影響を及ぼしている。トランプ大統領を支持するMAGA 派には、労
働所得に依存する低中所得層も多く含まれる。トランプ政権の下でも生活が改善しないと感じ
る有権者の不満は、政権への評価を押し下げている。例えば、トランプ大統領の支持率から不
支持率を差し引いたネット支持率は低下しており、連動する消費者センチメントも悪化してい
る（図表 4 左図）。分野別の支持動向をみても、生活コストと経済に対する評価が低い（図表 4
右図） 。つまり、現在の米国は経済全体が一定の成長を維持していても、その成長の恩恵がAI
関連企業、高所得層、金融資産保有者に偏っているため、低中所得層は景気の良さを実感しに

図表 4 消費者マインドとトランプ大統領の支持動向、分野別トランプ大統領の支持動向

（出所）Silver Bulletin、ロイター/ミシガン大より大和総研作成

メインシナリオは、上院・下院ともに民主党が僅差で多数派 
こうした経済環境や世論動向を踏まえると、今回の中間選挙は共和党にとって逆風となる公
算が大きい。現時点におけるメインシナリオは、上下院ともに民主党が多数派を獲得するケー
スとなる。中間選挙の基本的な仕組みを確認すると、上院では100 議席のうち35 議席、下院で
は全435 議席が改選される。上院は非改選議席を含めて51 議席、下院は218 議席を確保した政
党が多数派となる（図表5） 。 
特に下院については民主党優勢との見方が強い。下院は全議席が 2 年ごとに改選されるため、
政権や景気に対する有権者の評価が比較的反映されやすい。過去の中間選挙でも、下院におけ
る大統領所属政党の議席増減は、大統領支持率との連動性が高い。足元のトランプ大統領の支
持率は低水準かつ低下傾向にあり、下院共和党候補にとって厳しい環境といえる。現在の世論
調査を踏まえれば、民主党がすでに 220 議席程度を固めており、多数派を共和党から奪還する
公算が大きい。もっとも、民主党が下院で圧倒的な多数を得るとまではみていない。激戦区の
半分を民主党が制したと仮定すれば、民主党の議席は228 議席程度となる。多数派に必要な218
議席は上回るものの、共和党に対するリードは大きくない。下院のメインシナリオは「民主党
への交代」だが、その中身は圧勝ではなく、比較的僅差の多数派と捉えるべきだろう。 
より注目されるのは上院だ。上院議員の任期は 6 年で、一度の選挙で改選されるのは全体の
約 3 分の 1 に限られる。このため、上院選挙は大統領支持率との相関が下院ほど強くなく、候
補者個人の知名度や州固有の政治事情に左右されやすい。現時点の票読みでは、民主党が48 議

見れば極めて僅差であり、少数の激戦州の結果が多数派を決める。 
図表 5 上下院の議席構成・予想値

（注）2026 年10 月2 日時点。上院民主党には、無所属民主党会派も含む。上院に関しては、民主党は改選13 議
席、共和党は改選 22 議席。下院の現状における「激戦」は欠員を示す。 「有利」は“Likely”と“Leans”の合
計。 
（出所）270toWin より大和総研作成

それにもかかわらず、上院でも民主党が僅差で多数派を握るとみる理由として、共和党が今
回の選挙をトランプ大統領と強く結びつけてきたことが挙げられる2。9 月の共和党大会で、ト
ランプ大統領は、自身が出馬しているかのように共和党候補へ投票してほしいと訴えた。また、
激戦州の共和党候補にはMAGA 派や保守系の親トランプ派も見られ、有権者は候補者とトランプ
大統領のイメージを重ねやすい。この戦略は、トランプ大統領の支持率が高ければ共和党候補
への追い風となる一方、支持率が低迷する局面では逆風を上院選挙にも波及させうる。激戦州
の世論調査でも、MAGA 派候補は総じて苦戦している（図表6）。共和党がジョージア州やノース
カロライナ州よりも、立て直しの余地があるとみられるカンザス州やアイオワ州に選挙資金を
振り向け始めたとされ、激戦州とされる選挙区の中でも実際には民主党の勝利が近づいている
州もあると考えられる。 
ただし、上院で民主党が圧勝すると考えるのも早計だ。民主党側のリスクは、急進左派候補
が無党派層や「共和党も民主党も支持したくない」と考えるダブルヘイター層の票を十分に取
り込めない可能性といえる。民主党のコア支持層には歓迎される政策であっても、中道的な有
権者には投票をためらわせる場合がある。こうした取りこぼしを考慮すれば、上院のメインシ
ナリオは「民主党圧勝」ではなく、 「民主党が僅差で多数派を確保する」というものになる。

図表 6 上院選挙の注目州動向

（注）10 月 5 日時点の直近世論調査 3 つ（選挙区によって異なる）の平均値。共和党候補者の濃い赤色は MAGA
派、薄いピンクは保守・親トランプ派、白色は中道右派。民主党候補者の濃い青は急進左派、薄い青はリベラ
ル派、白色は中道左派。民主党優勢度は濃い青は民主党が明確に優勢、薄い青はやや優勢、濃いオレンジは共
和党が明確に優勢、薄いオレンジは共和党がやや優勢。 
（出所）Silver Bulletin より大和総研作成

中間選挙の注目点は民主党の「勝ち方」 、共和党の「負け方」 
上下院で民主党優勢との見方がコンセンサスになりつつあるが、最大の注目点は民主党の
「勝ち方」と共和党の「負け方」だ。仮に民主党が大勝した場合、2028 年の大統領選挙・議会
選挙に向けて共和党が勢力を立て直すハードルは高まる。たとえ共和党が大統領選挙で勝利し
たとしても、議会で民主党優位が続けば政権運営は容易ではない。一方、民主党にとっては今
回の中間選挙で大勝できれば、2028 年の議会選挙に対する不安を和らげることができるほか、
大統領選挙にも勝利した場合には、ホワイトハウスと上下両院を押さえる「オールブルー」に
近づく可能性が高まる。 
例えば、2028 年の大統領選挙・議会選挙も考慮した場合の上院の注目州については、ジョー
ジア州、テキサス州、オハイオ州、ミシガン州、メイン州、フロリダ州が挙げられる。共和党
にとって特に重要なのはテキサス州、オハイオ州、フロリダ州だ。テキサス州の上院選で共和
党が敗北すれば 1993 年の補欠選挙以来約 33 年ぶりとなり、トランプ大統領の求心力に疑問を
持つ共和党議員が増える可能性がある。また、オハイオ州はバンス副大統領の地盤であるため、
共和党候補を勝利に導けるかどうかは、バンス副大統領の党内求心力を測る材料となる。 
フロリダ州はトランプ大統領とルビオ国務長官の地盤であり、現状では共和党がおおむね優
勢とみられる。仮に共和党がオハイオ州で敗れる一方、フロリダ州を維持すれば、2028 年大統
領選挙を見据えた共和党内の主導権争いで、ルビオ国務長官の存在感が相対的に高まる可能性
